का . मा . 2657: -- तोटरीज नियम , 1956 के नियम 6 
के अनुसरण में सक्षम प्राधिकारी द्वारा यह सूचना दी जाती 
है कि श्री राम प्रकाश गोविला , एडवोकेट ने उक्त प्राधिकारी 
को उक्त नियम के नियम 4 के अधीन एक प्रावेदन 
इस बात के लिए दिया है कि उसे अलीगढ़ ( उत्तर प्रदेश ) 
में व्यवसाय करने के लिए नोटरी के रूप में नियुक्ति पर किसी 
भी प्रकार की आक्षेप इस सूचना के प्रकाशन के चौदह दिन 
के भीतर लिखित रूप से मेरे पास भेजा जाए ।

अनुसरण में सक्षम प्राधिकारी द्वारा यह सूचना दी जाती है 
है कि श्री प्रोद्योत कुमार घोष , एडवोकेट ने उक्त प्राधिकारी 
को उक्त नियम के नियम 4 के अधीन एक आवेदन इस 
बात के लिए दिया है कि उसे चन्दर नगर सब डिवीजन 
( पश्चिम बंगाल ) में व्यवसाय करने लिए नोटरी के रूप में नियुक्ति 
पर किसी भी प्रकार का आक्षेप इस सूचना के प्रकाशन के 
चौदह दिन के भीतर लिखित रूप से मेरे पास भेजा जाए ।

के अनुसरण में सक्षम प्राधिकारी द्वारा यह सूचना दी जाती अनुसरण में सक्षम प्राधिकारी द्वारा यह सूचना दी जाती है 
है कि श्री घनश्याम लाल पुरोहित , एडवोकेट ने उक्त प्राधिकारी फि श्री डी . जी . प णानी, एडवोकेट ने उक्त प्राधिकारी 
को उक्त नियम के नियम 4 के अधीन एक आवेदन इस बात को उक्त नियम के नियम 4 के अधीन एक आवेदन इस बात 
के लिए दिया है कि उसे किशन गढ जिला अजमेर ( राजस्थान ) के लिए दिया है कि उसे बम्बई ( एस . एस . एस . न .. र ) 
में व्यवसाय करने के लिए नोटरी के रूप में नियुक्ति पर किसी महाराष्ट्र में व्यवसाय करन के लिए नाटरी के रूप में निक्ति 
भी प्रकार का प्राक्षेप इस सूचना के प्रकाशन के चौदह दिन पर किता भी प्रकार आ आक्षेप इस सूचना के प्रकाशन के 
के भीतर लिखित रूप से मेरे पास भेजा जाए ।

का . प्रा . 266 5: - विस्थापित व्यक्ति ( प्रतिका एवं पुनर्वान ) 
अधिनियम 1954 ( 1954 का 44 ) की धारा 34 की 
उप धारा ( 1 ) द्वारा प्रदत्त शक्तियों का प्रयोग करते हुए 
केन्द्र सरकार एतदद्वारा निदेश देती है कि उत्तर प्रदेश राज्य 
में क्षतिपूर्ति पूल के भाग के रुप में भूमि एवं सम्पत्तियों के 
सम्बन्ध में उक्त अधिनियम की धारा 33 के अन्तर्गत इसके 
द्वारा प्रयोग की जा सकने वाली किन्ही भी शक्तियों का 
10- 4-92 से उत्तर प्रदेश राज्य सरकार के राजस्व विभाग 
में संयुक्त सचिव द्वारा भी उनके स्वयं के दायित्वों के प्रति 
रिक्त प्रयोग किया जा सकेगा ।

MINISTRY OF HOME AFFAIRS

( Rehabilitation Division ) 

Naw Dolbi, the 7th August, 1992 
s. o . 2665 . . - In exercise of the powers conferred by sub. 
section (i ) of Section 34 of the Displaced Persons ( Com 
pensation and Rehabilitation) Act , 1954 ( 44 of 1954 ) the 
Cedral Government hereby directs that any powers exercis 
able by it under Sectica 33 of the said Act shall be exercisable 
also by the Joint Secretary in the Revenue Department of the 
State Government of Uttar Pradesh , in addition to his own 
duties , in respect of the lands & properties forming part of the 
Compensation Pool within the State of Uttar Pradesh w .e .t. 
10th April, 1992.

Cell / 88- SS.II dated 7th April , 1989.

नई दिल्ली , 26 अगस्त , 1982 


सम्पूर्ण महाशक्तियों से दिल्ली विश 


का . मा . 2666: - विस्थापित व्यक्ति ( प्रतिभार एवं पूनर्वास ) 
अधिनियम 1954 ( 1954 का 44 ) की धारा 3 द्वारा 
प्रवत शक्तियों का प्रयोग करते हुए केन्द्र सरकार एतदद्वारा 
तहसीलदार याऊन्धी सेल , कार्यालय वितायक्त ( राजस्व ) 
पंजाब सरकार जिन्हें पुनर्वास विभाग पंजाब सरकार द्वारा 
प्रबन्ध अधिकारी के रूप में सभी अर्धन्यायिक मामले सौंप 
दिए गए हैं , को विस्थापित व्यक्तियों द्वारा पाकिस्तान में 
छोड़ दी गई भूमि के बदले में ग्रामीण निष्क्रान्त कृषि भूमि का 
पाबंटन प्राप्त करने के लिए उनके अधिकारों की पात्रता का 
निर्धारण करने के सम्बन्ध में उक्त अधिनियम के द्वारा अथवा

of the Foreign Exchange Regulation Act, 1973, ( 46 of 1973 ) 
and in supersession of its Notifications No. FERA 3 / 74 -RB 
dated 1st January , 1974 and No . FERA 108 ,92 -RB dated 
15th February , 1992 , the Reserve Bank is pleased to direct 
that the prohibition imposed by that sub - section shall not 
apply to the maintenance of and operations on an account,

in India in accordance with any schemes approved by the 
Reserve Bank in this behalf, or outside India , if such foreign 
currency was acquired by a person ,

( b ) through employment, business or vocation outside 

India , taken up or commenced while such person was 

resident outside India ; 
Provided that in either case , such a person has been resi 
dent outside India for a continuous period of not less than 
one year.

The said referencc was registered as Industrial Dis 
pute No . 2 of 1992 on the file of this Tribunal.

Respondent put in appearance and one Advocate by 
namic Sri K . Srinivas offered to appear for the Peti 
tiones and later he did not file vakalat for the 
Petitioner and the Petitioner remained exparte .

mont setting forth the claims. The Respondent also 
did not file counter and the Respondent filed a 
Memo stating that the Union has not evinced any 
interest to filc its claims statement and as such the 
Management will not he in a position to file any 
counter and that this Hon ble Court may be pleased 
to pass a nil award ,

prosecute his case and the Petitioner did not appear

· AND

before the Tribunal and did not file any claim state 
ment and he also did not choose to adduce any 
evidence before this Tribunal. Likewise thc Res 
pondent also did not file any counter and did not 
choose to adduce any evidence on its behalf. In 
view of the conduct of the parties, there is no 
material brought on record to establish their res 
pective case and there is no material available on 
record to decide the matter on merits . Under the 
circumstances of the case , it cannot be said that 
the Petitioner has established his case in respect of 
his claim as referred to this Tribunal for adjudica 
tion in the reference. Therefore , I hold that the 
petitioner is not entitled for any relief in this case . 

. In the result , an Award is passed holding that 
the concerned workman Sri M . Venkaty , Grade I 
Clerk is not entitled for any relief in this case . There 
will be no order as to costs .

BEFORE THE INDUSTRIAL TRIBUNAL AT

INDUSTRIAL DISPUTE NO. 61 OF 1990 


It is submitted that the Petitioner was appointed as 
Watchman on daily wage rate basis . The Food Cor 
poration of India is engaged in procurement of paddy 
in Andhra Pradesh . During 1973 to 1976 there wa 
a heavy procurement programme consequent on bum 
per crop in Andhra Pradesh . However, recruitment

THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : OCTOBEP. 17, 199 ? !ASVINA 25, 1914 


(PART II SEC . 3 ü ) ] 


above discussion , I am of opinion , that the petitioner 
could not establish his case that he was not given 
opportunity by the Respondents to offer himself for 
the re - employment in the same post from which he 
was terminated. Therefore , I am cf opinion , that 
it cannot be said that the Respondents did not give 

is in haar wer esponent notre 
an opportunity in terms of Section 25 - H of the I. D . 
Act to the Petitioner . In view of the mandatory 
provisions of Section 25 - H , I am of opinion , that it 
is reaçonable to direct the second Respondent to give 
an opportunity to the Petitioner for re - employment 
if the second Respondent proposes to employ any 
watchman on daily rate wages at hiired godowns in 
future. Hence I answer the point accordingly . 


7. In the result , an Award is passed holding that 
thc Petitioner failed to establish that he second Res 
pondent cmployed any watchman on daily rate wages 
for hired godowns, subsequent to the date of peti 
tions s termination from service and that the second 
Respondent did not give him an opportunity for re 
employment in such appointments , if any, made by 
the Second Respondent, as per the provisions of 
Section 25 -H of the Industrial Disputes Act. How 
ever , the second Respondent is directed to give an 
opportunity to the petitioner for re - employment in 
future as per the provisions of Secion 25 - H of the 
I. D . Act , in case he proposes to employ anv watch 
man on daily rate wages for hired godowns, I hold 
that the Politioner is not entitled for any relief 
against the first Respondent. There will be no order 
as to the costs under the facts and circumstances 
of the case .

It is submitted that the petitioner is a Trade Union 
have been registered under thc Trade Union Act. 
The worknian fiumbering 38 mentioned in the 
annexure to the reference members of the Petitioner

unloading operations of the F . C . I. at Mandanapalli 
Depot for thic last several years . It is submitted that 
the F . C .I. is having a modern rice mill at Miryalguda 
in Nalgonda District where food grains are stocked . 
For the purpose of loading and unloading opera 
tious, thc F . C . I. engages hamals at local level. The 
said 38 workmen were working as Hamals who were 
employed for loading and unloading of stocks of lood 
grains at Mandanapalli near Aler. The said godown 
was established in the year 1976 and cver since the 
establishmçnt of the godown at Mandanapalli , bo 
workmcn concerned in the dispute have been working 
as Hanials. The payments are made by the F . C . I. 
directly or soinetimes through artificially created inter 
mediary agency. The workmen were being paid on 
piece - rate basis. As per procedure which is being 
followed in the F . C . I, the Hammals are engaged at 
local level where godowos are situato and they are 
being engaged continuously throughout the year , 
The piece rate system is being adopted only in the 
case of the work men engaged by the F . C . I. as 
Hamals for loading and unloading operations. 
These workmen have been permanent y attached to 
the godowns and thev were engaged for the handling 
and transport operations ever since 1976 . All the 
workmen concerned are engaged in the F . C I and 
therefore , they would come within the definition of 
workmen under the I. D . Act. While the natter 
stood thus, they were disengaged from 2 - 1 - 1985 . 
The Respondents have not given any notice nor paid 
any retrenchment comnerisation as required under 
the provisions of tho I. D . Act. Since they fulfil the 
definition of workmen and they have bern working 
continuously for the last several vers , from 1976 
onwards, their services connot be terminaict without 
giring any notice as required under Section 25 - F of 
the I. D . Act. Therefore , the action of the manane . 
mant ir disengaoing the workmen froni 2 - 1- 1985 
amounts to retrenchment within the meaning of 
Section 100 ) of the L. D . Act and conspirently 
their fermination is to be held illeogl for vinlarinn of 
Section 25- F of the LD , Act. It to Go- ther shinittari 
that the workmen were working directly under the 
ICT and the emnlover being the TCI. the reg

The present dispute has been raised by the union 
after a lapse of ten years by way of gambling in liti 
gation . The demand of the union is speculative one 
and the present dispute has been made after a long 
delay . Hence , this crispute is not maintainable . There 
was shortage of Telephone Operators in the Com 
pany. Therefore, an internal notice was circulated 
inviting applications from interested candidates who 
had prescribed qualification for the post of Telcphone 
Operator and Telephone Operator ( Trainee ) . The 
concerned workmen made application and on the 
recommendations of the Selection Coinmittee they 
were given offer as Telephone Operator ( Trainee ) and 
placed in their existing scale of pay . After comple 
tion of training period they were regularised as Tele 
phone Operator and placed in Clerical Grade Il which 
was the minimum prescribed grade of the Telephone 
Operators, M . L . Roy was working at Simlabahal 
colliery of Kustore Area as Tub -Checker in Clcrical 
Grade- II and R . P . Singh was working at Kustore 
Collicry of Kurtore Arca as Cap Lamp Fitter in Cate 
gory - II ( Jaiy rated ) . Both of them were regularised 
in Clerical Gade- II which was much highe grade than 
their cxisting cale of pay . They were appointed as 
Telephone Operator in 1982 . At the time of their 
appointment as Telcphone Operators the Cadre Scheme 
formulated by the managenient on 20 -6 - 77 was in 
operation and both of them were rightly placed in 
Clerical Grade- II as per the said Cadre Scheme. The 
demand of the union for placement of M . L , Roy and 
R . P . Singh as Telephone Operator in Clerical Grade 
I with effect from 16 - 3 -81 to 8 - 9 -87 is not justified . 

4 . Jo rejoindor to the written statement of the 
management, the union has asserted that the present 
dispute is maintainable . It is not a fact that thic con 
cerned workmen were working as Telephone Opera 
tor ( Trainee ) . But the Office Order clearly indicates 
that they worked as Telephone Operator from 
16 - 3 -81. As both of them have been working in 
the post of Teleplione Operator continuously with 
effect from 16 - 3 -81 they entitled to be placed in 
Clerical Grade - I. 

. . In rejoinder to the written statement of the 
sponsoring union , the management has denied and 
disputed the contentions of the union and struck io 
the position as appearing in its written statement. 

6 . The union , in order to prop up its claim , has 
examined the concerned worknian as WW - 1 and 
WW - 2 and laid in evidence a number of documents 
which have been marked Fxts . W - 1 to W - 12 . 

On the other hand , the management bas cxamined 
only one witness , namely , MW - 1 R . N . Mitra , posted 
as Dy , Personnel Manager at Hcad Quarters of M /s . 
B . C . C . Ltd . in Koyla Bhawan and laid in evidence 
somc documents which lave been marked Exts . M - 1 
to M - 3 . 


in its written statement or rejoinder. Shri Roy has 
admutted in his evidence that he was working in Simla 
bahal colliery as Tub - checker in Clerical Grade- III . 

Vihough R . P . Singh has admitted that he was work 
ing as Helper to Cap Lamp Fitter, he has claimed that 
he was placed in Category - IV . This claim of Shri 
Singh scems to be a tall claim as Fitter Helpers are 
placed in Category - li both as per Wage Board Recom 
mendations and N . C . W . A . 

8. Anyway, the fact is that the managenient called 
both the concerned workinen , M .L , Roy by letter 
dated 12 - 12 -79 : 21 ; 1180124 - 1 - 80 (Ext. W . 1 ) and 
R . P , Singh by letter dated 12 - 12- 7921 - 1 - 80 ( Ext. 
W -11) for an interview for training as Telex Operator 
at Koyla Bhawan on 31 - 1 -80 . Both of them appeared 
in the interview and selected for training as Telex 
Operator for a period of six months. The letters of 
the management dated 5 - 7 - 80 (Ext. W - 2 ) and 8 - 4 -80 
2 - 5 - 80 (Ext. W - 2 / 1 ) indicate this position . There is 
no dispute that both the concerned workmen under 
went training successfully . By Office Order dated 
14 | 16 - 3 - 81 both of them were deployed for shift duty 
as Telephone Operator ( l rainee) . Admittedly , the 
concerned workmen were getting emoluments as per 
their previous scale of pay obtaining prior to their 
joining as Telephone Operator ( Trainee ) (Ext. W - 3 ) . 

9 . The management by Office Order dated 14 -5 - 82 
(Ext. W - 4 ) promoted the concerned workmen as 
Telephone Operator in Clerical Grade - II . The union 
has stated that the management regularised the services 
of the concerned workmen in Clerical Grade - I in 
terms of circular dated 7 - 4 -77. According 
lothic union , this circular , is unconstitutional. 
There is nothing on record to indicate that 
the management issued any circular dated 
7 -41-77 on the subject. On the other hand , the manuge 
went has asserted ihat & the tinc of appointment of 
the concerned workmen as Telephone Operator , Cadec 
Scheme formulaled by Office Order dated 20 - 6 - 77 
was in operaſion and they were rightly placed in Cleri 
val Grade- II as per Cadra Scheme. The manager:ent 
has produced the Cadre Scheme dated 20 -6 - 77 ( Ext. 
M - 2 ) on the subject of promotion policy of ministerial 
cadre. Bu * this circular does not seem to be a complete 
one. Tliis circular cnvisages that promotion to Clerical 
Grade- I will be made areawise or Headquarters -wise 
by D . P . C . and promotion to Clerical Grade- [ will be 
made colliury -wise . are : -wise and direcionale -wire . 
The union has adinitted in its written statement that 
as per circular of the management da’ ed 7 - 4 - 77 ( Ext 
W - 5 ) an employee who is a matriculate and capable 
of operating PBX having more than 10 internal ex 
tensice lines are entiteu tu be placed in Clerical 
Grade- I. Admitlcdly , both the concerned workmien 
ere nistriculates ( Exis . W - S and W -11) . But there 
is no cvidence on Iccord that both of them were 
capable of operating PBX having more than 10 internal 
extension lines. This being the position , I am CODS 
trained to hold that the claim of the union for placing 
the concerned workmen in Cleric :11 Grade- T with 
cffect from 16 - 3 - 81 is not tepable .

ten statenient -cum -rejoinder that M .L . Roy , one of 
the concerned workman , was working in Simlabahal 
colliery of Kustore Arca di Tuh -checker in Clerical 
Grade- III and R . P . Singh , the other concerned work 
mun , was working at Kustore colliery of Kustore Area 
its ( 2p Lip Bitter in Category - II. These statenicuts 
of cis five not been assailed by the sponsoring union

from the post of Telephone Operator in Technical

Grado D which corresponds to Clerical Grade - II 
with effect from 9 - 9 - 87 (Ext. W - 5 ) . In the meantime 
the management issued another circular dated 17 - 12 - 85

phonc personnels : Telephonc Operator to Senior 
Telephone Operator Grade- I. As per this circular 
Teisphone Operator in Technical Grade D having

equivalent qualification are entitled to be promoted 
to the post of Senior Telephone Operator Grade- II in 
Technical Grade C . Both the concerned workmen

that the concerned workmen for placement in Clerical

Operator Grade - II in Technical Grade C which

automatically follows that they are also entitled to 
difference of wages from 17 - 12 - 85 till they are 
actually placed in Technical Grade C ".

M . L . Roy and R . P . Singh as Telehone Operator in

ponds to Clcrical Grade - I with effect from 17 - 12 - 85 
and difference of wages from that date till they are 
actually promoted to the grade aforesaid .

This is my award .

2 . In this case both the parties appeared and filed 
their respective W .S . documents. Thereafter the case 
proceeded along its course . Subsequently at the stage 
of oral evidence both the parties appeared before me 
and filed a Joint Compromise petition under their 
signature . I heard both the parties on the said peti 
tion of compromise and I do find that the terms 
contained therein are fair , proper and beneficial to 
both of them . Accordingly I accept the said petition 
of compromise and pass an Award in terms thereof 
which forms part of the Award as Annexure ,

Joint compromise petition of the employers and 
workman 
The above mentioned cmployers and the workman 
beg to submit jointly as follows : 

1 . That the matter covered by the above reference 
was mutually negotiated between the employers and 
thc workman with a view to arrive at an amicable 
and mutually acceptable overall settlement. 

2 . That as a result of such negotiation , the em 
ployers and the workman have agreed to settle the 
dispute on the following terms and conditions : 
( i) that Sri K . I. Robi, P . No . 9702 , Attendance 

Clerk , South Mine, Chasnalla Colliery , shall 
be placed in Clerical Grade - I with effect 
from 1 - 9 - 1983. Since he is working in Time 
Section for the last 17 years, he will be 
designated as Senior Time Keeper, South 
Mino (Upper Seam ) , Chaspalla Colliery , 
with effect from 1 - 9 - 1983 and this post shall 

remain personol to him . 
( i ) that he shall not claim any wages being the 

difference between Clcrical Grade - I and 
Clerical Grade- II for the period between 

31 - 8 - 1983 to 30 -4 - 1992 , 
( c ) due incremental benefits shall be given to 

Sri Robi while arriving at his present basis 

as on 1 May, 1992 . 
( d ) that this is an overall settlement in full and 

final settlement of all the claims of the 

workman arising out of the above reference , 
3 . That the employers and the workman consider 
and hereby declare that the above terms and conditions 
of settlement are just fair and reasonable to both the 
parties. 

The employers and the workman , therefore , jointly 
pray that the Hon ble Tribunal may be pleased to 
accept this joint compromisc petition and give an 
award in terms thereof and dispose off the reference 
accordingly .

not appear that the Enquiry Officer has made any 
endeavour to ascertain about truth or falsity of this 
fact in domestic enquiry. 
• 9 . The concerned workman was arraigned on

Clause 17 ( i) ( c ) .- Wilful insubordination or 

disobedience , whether alone or in conjunc 
tion with others or of any lawful or rea 

sonable order of a superior. 
Clause 17 (i) (f).- - Habitual or serious neglect 

of work . 
Clause 17 (i) (u ). -- Malingering or slowing 

down work . 
Clause 17 (i) ( r ). Threatening, abusing or 

assaulting any superior or co -worker. 
It appears that Clause 17 (i) (u ) does not envi 
sage malingering or slowing down work as mis 
conduct ; it is Clause 17 (i ) (k ) which envisages that 
provision . Anyway , I think that the concerned 
workman was not prejudiced in his defence by 
erroneous quotation of the aforesaid clause in the 
charge- sheet . . 
. 10 . The Enquiry Officer exonerated the concerned

my view correctly . Nevertheless , he found the con 
cerned workman guilty of the charges under Clause 
17 (i) (c ) and 17 (i) (r ) of the Certified Standing

Rajiv Ranjan Kumar , Under Manager, stated that 
Sri Midya told the concerned workinan to perform 
his assigned duty but he refused to do so . Thus, 
the evidence of these witnesses contradicts each 
other regarding the nature of job allotted to the 
concerned workman . Sri Mukherjee stated that 
he was allotted the job of hanging ventilation tube 
while Sri Midya stated that he asked the concerned 
workman to do the job . of cleaning and Rajiv 
Ranjan kurnar stated that Sri Midya asked him to 
perform the job assigned to him by the Overman . 
The Overnian assigned him the job of hanging 
ventilation tube. Considering serious inconsistency 
in the cvidence of the management, ? 5 pointed out 
above, I have no hesitation to hold that the charge 
against the concerned workman under Clause 
17 ( i) ( c ) of the Certified Standing Orders for nist 
conduct of wilful insubordination or disobedience to 
carry out the order of the superior or wilful insub 
ordination of any lawful order or reasonable order 
of a superior is not sustainable . 

12 . Now , remains the charge of misconduct under 
Clause 17 (i ) ( r ) for threatening , abusing or assault 
ing any superior or co -worker . Sri Midya has 
stated that when he asked the concerned workman 
to do the job of cleaning he abused him (Midya ) 
in filthy language and attempted to assault him , 
but in the meantime Rajiv Ranjan Kumar, Under 
manager and T . K . Mukherjee , Overman reached 
the place and took the concerned workman away. 
T . K . Mukherite stated that he saw Sri Ghosh and 
Midya catching the hands of each other and he 
separated them and he went to the face with Sri 
Midya . Sri Mukherjee did not support Sri Midya 
that the concerned woikian abused and attempted 
to assault Sri Midya . Rajiv Ranjan Kumar , Under 
manager , stated that Sri Midya asked him to accom 
pany him in the matter of inspection . Sri Midya 
has not supported Sri Kumar on this point. Any 
way , Sri Kumar has stated that the concerned work 
man abused Sri Midya and tried to manhandle him . 
I have also stated that Sri Mukherjec did not sup 
port the statement of Sri Midya that the concerned 
workman abused and attempted to assault Sri Midya . 
The concerned workman , it appears from his expla 
nation , submitted a complaint of his having been 
assaulted by Sri Midya . Nobody knows whether 
that complaint was enquired into or not. The 
Enquiry Officer has not made any enquiry in the 
matter . In such circumstances, Sri Midya had an 
animus against the concerned workman . The evi.. 
dence on record against the concerned workman for 
his having abused and attempted to assault Sri 
Midya is very slander and inconsistent and hence , 
I do not consider it wise to held the concerned 
worknian guilty of the charge by relying on such 
slander and inconsistent evidence . 

13 . I come to the conclusion that the charge 
against the concerned workman has not been proved 
by the evidence on record , This being so , the order 
of dismissal of the concerned workman from ser 
vice present by the moncomment on the basis of the 
report of the Enquiry Officer who found him guilty 


.

Overnan reported the matter to A . K . Midya , Sr .

concerned workman to carry out the job of cleaning 
allotted to luin , but he refused to carry out the job .

Project of M !s . B . C .C . Ltd . in disinissing S . P . 
Ghosh , General Mazdoor by order of dismissal dated 
4 - 5 - 1987 . is not justified . The order of dismissal is

THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : OCTOBER 17, 1992 / ASVINA 25 , 1914 


[PART II - SEC. 3 ( ii) ; 


Area . His letter further discloses that after enquiry 
he was sent to the Central Hospital, Baghmara . on 
22 - 9 - 87 where the doctor declared that it would take 
soinetime. ( io recover ) . He requested the Inspector 
to take necessary step and promosed to abide by his 
order . 

9 . It appears that since no head - way was made 
in the matter Janta Mazdoor Sangh espoused the 
cause of the concerned workman by raising an indus 
trial dispute over the alleged wrongful stoppage of 
work of the concerned workman and solicited inter 
vention of the Conciliation Officer in the matter (Ext. 
M - 2 ) . It further appears that the dispute was ami 
cably settled in presence of the Conciliation Officer 
on the following terms : 
" O) That Shri Ram Eqbal Singh , Night Guard 

shall be allowed to resume his duties with 

immediate effect. : ) 
( 2 ) That the period of his absence w . e.f. 

16 - 9 -87 till resumption of his duties will be 

treated as leave without pay . 
( 3 ) That the management and the Union , both 

shall submit their implementation report to 
the KLC ( C ) , Dhanbad and the ALC ( C ) , 
Dhanbad - II within fifteen days, failing 
which it will be presumed that the settle 
ment in question , has been implemented in 

full. ” 
This settlement has been assailed by the concerned 
workraan on two grounds - firstly the concerned 
workman gave no authority to Janta Mazdoor Sangh 
to raise the dispute on his behalf ; and secondly, that 
the settlement is not legal and valid . 

Shri S. Bose , authorised representative of the 
sponsoring union , Rashtriya Colliery Mazdoor Sangh , 
which has raised the present dispute has contended 
that the concerned workman was never a member of 
Janta Mazdoor Sangh . The concerned workman has 
stated so in his testimony before this Tribunal. The 
management in its written statement has stated that 
the present dispute was amicably settled between the 
management and Janta Mazdoor Sangh on 30 - 12 - 87 
in the course of conciliation proceeding. The con 
cerned workman has stated in his rejoinder that it is 
absolutely incorrect to say that the present dispute 
was settled between the management and Janta Maz 
door Sangh on 30 - 12 - 87 . He has not disputed 
therein the fact that he was a member of Santa Maz 
door Sangh at the relevant time. The settlement 
(Ext. M - 2 ) erivisages that both the management 
and the union should submit Implementation Report 
to the R . L . C . ( C ) . In terms of the settlement the 
management by letter dated 22 - 1 -88 directed the 
concerned workman to report for duty immediately 
( Ext M - 3 ) . In reply to that letter the concerned 
workman by letter dated 15 - 2 -88 (Ext . W -13) stat 
ed that he was not aware of any settlement arrived 
at between the management and . Janta Mazdoor 
Sangh and that he was a member of Rashtriya Colliery 
Mazdoor Sangh and Janta Mazdoor Sangh had no 
right to raise industrial dispute on his behalf . 
Ile expressed his willingness therein to join his 
sluty . The disclaimer of the concerned workman of 
ais not being a member of Janta Mazdoor Sangh 


seems to be an afterthought. It is very difficult to 
believe as to how Janta Mazdoor Sangh would raise 
the dispute on his behalf unless the concerned work 
man was their member . The concerned workman 
has acuutted in cross - examination that he did not 
complain against Janta Mazdoor Sangh before Asstt. 
Labour Commissioner ( C ) , R .L . C . ( C ) and the Minis 
try . Considering the evidence on record , I am cons 
trained to hold that the concerned workman raised 
an industrial dispute through the agency of Janta 
Mazdoor Sangh before conciliation Officer which ulti 
timately ended in a settlement dated 30 - 12 -87 in pre 
sence of the Conciliation Officer ( Ext. M -2 ) . 

Shri B . Joshi, learned Advocate for the manage 
ment, has submitted that the settlement does not pro 
vide for payment of wages to the concerned workman 
during the period of his absence from duties and 
hence he has disclaimed to be a member of Janta 
Mazdoor Sangh and thereby tried to scuttle the 
settlement. The contention of Shri Joshi cannot be 
dismissed out of hand . 

10 . The next contention of Shri Bose is that the 
settlement is not legal and valid as the Conciliation 
Officer did not send a report of the conciliation pro 
ceeding together with a copy of memorandum of 
settlement signed by the parties to the dispute to the 
Central Government as Rule 58 of Indusrial Dis 
putes (Central) Rules , 1957 envisages . This sub 
mission of Shri Bose does not impress me at all. 
Neither the sponsoring union , Rashtriya Colliery 
Mazdoor Sangh nor the concerned workman has call 
ed for record from the office of the Conciliation OM 
cer to prove the fact that provisions of Rule 58 of 
Industrial Disputes (Central) Rules, 1957 had not 
been complied with . Even so the Conciliation Offi 
cer might have made breach of duty by not comply 
ing with the provision of Rule 58 aforesaid but that 
does not effect the legality of the conciliation settle 
ment. ( 4 S .C .L . J . 2365 between State of Bihar VS . 
Kripa Shankar Jaiswal ) . The settlement is still bind 
ing upon the parties to the settlement as there is no 
evidence that the concerned workman gave a notice 
in writing to terminate the settlement. This being 
the position , I hold that the present industrial dispute 
is not maintainable in view of the earlier settlement. 

11. Accordingly , the following award is rendered -- 
the action of the management of Lohapati Colliery 
of Ms. B . C . C . Ltd . in giving effect to the settlement 
dated 30 - 12 -87 in terms of which the concerned 
workman was to resume his duty immediately and 
the period of his absence from duty was to be treated 
as leave without pay is justified . The concerned 
worknian is directed to report for duty immediately 
and the nianagement is directed to allow him to 
resume duty . 
In the circumstances of the case I award no cost .

3 -4 -84 itself . The subinission definitely seemd to 
be correct but it not necessary that the pos al receipt 
( Ext. W - 15 ) must be relating to the letter of illness ., 
I have already pointed out that there were a num 
ber of discrepancies in the evidence of WW -2 . I have 
carefully perused the Sl. No . of the receints which 
are on chronological order as per date noted above.

such receipts as forged paper. 


tunity to the employer to adduce evidence before 
ii. it was pointed that there was no provision 
in the statute of the act stating that the order 
of dismissal or discharge was jilegai if it was 
not proceeded by proper and valid domes ?ic enquiry . 
I would like to mention that the provision is with 
respect to dismissal or discharge of a workman . Here 
in the instant case we find that Ram Janam Rani 
was terminated from his service which can be 
found from Ext. M - 3 . Even on the letter of request 
Ext. M - 4 there is an endorsement of an officer of 
the management that Ram Janam Ram has already 
heun terminated . The word " termination " is cqui 
valent to retrenchment. Section 2 (00 ) defines rc 
trenchment meaning termination by the employer of 
the services of workincn for any reason whatsoever 
other than cxcepted therein . This means the ser 
vices of Ram Janam Ram was terminated and that 
being the position the requirement of Section 25F 
will definitely come to pay. It provides that no work 
man employed in any industry who has been in conti 
nuous services for not less than one year under en 
employer shall be refrenched by that employer untill 

( a ) the workman has bccri given one month s notice 
in writing indicating the reason of retrenchment and 
thie period of notice has expired , or the workman 
has been paid in lieu of such notice the wages for 
the period of notice . The learned counsel for the 
work men has placed his reliance upon the authority 
reported in LIC Part II 1984 at page 1051 , Non 
comisliance of the provision of Section 25F will 
definitely vitiate such termination and the cmployec 
will be deemed to be in continuous service . Again 
continuous service has already been dofiucd under 
Section 25B of the Y. D) , Act, 1947. As per terms of 
the definition Shri Ram Janam Ram had completed 
conjouous service for a year and he will be deemed 
to have cxpired while under the enployment.

the learned counsel for the management relied upon 
the most important decision reported in ILJ - VOII 
1973 page 278 ( workmen of Firestone and Rubher

that as held by their Lordship it was not very neces 
sary to hold enquiry on os herore passing of any

aven if no enguiry has been held by an employer or 
if the enquiry held by him is found to he leflective , 
the Tribunt in order to satisfy itself about the 
legality and validity of the ordor has to give oppor 


14. After having considered this aspect on the 
matter I am to hold the view that Shri [ alti Devi 
wife of late Ram Tanam Ram is entitled for einploy 
ment as dependent of her deceased husband under 
the provision of 0 .4 3 of NCWA - II ) , The provision 
provides an employincol to one dependent of a wor 
ker who is permanently disabled in his place . The 
disablement of the worker concerned should arise 
forin injury or disease , be of permanent nature re 
sulting into loss of employment and it should be so 
certificd by the Coal Company concerned. Here in 
the instant casc we find tha : the concerned workman 
had become completely invalid on account of his 
ajlment and therefore he was disabled and Lalti Devi 
the applicant is entitled for cmployment under the 
aforesaid provision . The management is thus direc 
ted to provide cmploynient to Smt. Lalti Devi in the 
collicry in the mirioium Cat, within one month froin 
the date of publication of the Award ,
